Chinese Glass Paintings in Bangkok Monasteries
page=2 block=2138701832 (x=98 y=1) width=333.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
jessica lee patterson
page=2 block=1725825240 (x=198 y=36) width=133.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFD+Advp102060 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
University of San Diego
page=2 block=1479606532 (x=211 y=52) width=107.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFE+AdvPN10205D wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Reverse glass paintings, a form of Chinese export art, were extensively traded in the nineteenth century. Several examples
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=117) width=526.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are on display in prominent Thai Buddhist monasteries in Bangkok. King Nangklao of Siam, Rama III, encouraged
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=130) width=527.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Sino-Siamese trade that brought Chinese objects and images to nineteenth-century Siam. The ideals of accretion and
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=143) width=527.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
abundance characteristic of Thai Buddhism and the sinophilia of Rama III facilitated the construction of ‘‘Chinese-
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=156) width=527.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
style’’ Thai temples. Glass paintings with scenes of the Pearl River Delta, Romance of the Three Kingdoms, auspicious
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=169) width=527.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
objects, and bird-and-?ower compositions were installed in temples and inspired new directions in Thai mural painting.
page=2 block=34545662 (x=1 y=182) width=527.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
keywords: glass paintings, reverse glass, Chinese export art, Thai temples, Buddhist monasteries, Thai Buddhism,
page=2 block=33691890 (x=1 y=206) width=514.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Rama III, King Nangklao, Third Reign, Sino-Siamese trade, Thai art, Samkok, Romance of the Three Kingdoms,

ities, sometimes funerary services and a place to enshrine
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=310) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the ashes of the cremated, as well as a place where living
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=323) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
community members can gather to hear the teachings,
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=336) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
honor their dead, participate in ceremonies, donate, and
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=349) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
engage in other forms of merit-making. At a minimum,
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=362) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the art and architecture of the monastery need only be
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=375) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
conducive to such ends, although the tendencies of Thai
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=388) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
art are anything but minimalistic. As Justin McDaniel
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=400) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
remarks in his insightful treatment of contemporary Thai
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=413) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
religious and visual culture, ‘‘Generally, in Thai Buddhism
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=426) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘more is more’. Shrines . .. are sites of accretion.’’ An
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=439) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
aesthetic of abundance (udom sombun ????????) is
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=451) width=252.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
highly valued.1
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=463) width=68.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In their more concrete manifestations, abundance
page=2 block=16621596 (x=19 y=478) width=234.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and accretion can take the form of manifold ornaments
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=491) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on and elaborations of the otherwise basic and boxy
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=504) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
architectural form of the image halls, the plethora of
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=517) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
images and offerings that crowd a typical altar, or the
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=530) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
complex and detailed composition of most mural paint-
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=543) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ings. But these principles of accretion and abundance
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=556) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
are not limited to the intricacy and horror vacui so
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=569) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
characteristic of Thai art: they can also account for the
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=582) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
presence of objects that, to eyes conditioned by the con-
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=595) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ventional categories of art history, might appear foreign,
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=608) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
secular, or otherwise out of place (Fig. 1).
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=621) width=188.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
One very distinctive example is the Chinese export
page=2 block=16621596 (x=19 y=634) width=234.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
art, primarily reverse glass paintings, that can be still be
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=647) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
found in a number of monasteries in Bangkok that were
page=2 block=16621596 (x=1 y=659) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
built or restored in the second quarter of the nineteenth
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=271) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
century (Fig. 2). As objects of foreign origin whose
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=284) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
presence is best explained by their appeal to the pre-
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=297) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
dilections of the temples’ patrons, these paintings can
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=310) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
certainly be considered accretions, but also, in their
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=323) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
own status as commodities of a lucrative international
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=336) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
trade and often in their imagery as well, they re?ect the
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=349) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
aesthetic of abundance that lends an air of auspicious-
page=2 block=263257074 (x=276 y=362) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ness to a monastery (Fig. 3).

of the nineteenth century forms a fascinating interlude

in the history of Thai art. The monasteries of this era are

???? ???????? (Chinese altar tables) and scenes from

the fourteenth-century novel Romance of the Three King-

Many of these are hybrid visual forms, Chinese imagery
page=2 block=263264126 (x=276 y=530) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adapted by Chinese-Thai artists for Thai religious

spaces. Some reverse glass paintings closely resemble

the new mural designs and, in some cases, may have

served as models or inspiration. This further complicates

imported directly from China, they were Sino-European

hybrids in style, substance, and technique.

(1824–51), inspiring new aesthetic directions in mural

painting and re?ecting the intimate relationships between

Siam and China before trade and diplomacy with Europe

of Chinese glass paintings in Thai monasteries is John

Clark in his Modern Asian Art (1998). Illustrating his

and imagery, ?t into McDaniel’s descriptions of a Thai

ARCHIVES OF ASIAN ART
page=9 block=385351684 (x=49 y=1) width=120.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
space provides an especially vivid case of the relativiza-

of cross-cultural transfer.
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=52) width=113.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The eminence of glass paintings in early-nineteenth-
page=9 block=16529684 (x=19 y=65) width=234.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
century Siam was very unusual given the broader history
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=78) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of this medium, one that in most times and places has
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=90) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
operated as a separate, distinctly inferior sphere of
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=103) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
image-making. Even in Siam glass paintings were col-
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=116) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
lected and displayed in the residences and temples of
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=129) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the wealthy for only a limited period. By the middle of
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=142) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the nineteenth century, Siam’s growing trade with the
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=155) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
West began to eclipse trade with China, and the new
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=168) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Western turn meant that Chinese designs were no longer
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=181) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as fashionable among Bangkok’s aristocracy as they had
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=194) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
been. Chinese art and imagery became less prominent
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=207) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
in temple and palace decoration, although it was never
page=9 block=16529684 (x=1 y=220) width=251.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
discontinued altogether.

Despite the many Chinese glass paintings imported

to Siam in the early nineteenth century, this art form

JESSICA LEE PATTERSON  Chinese Glass Paintings in Bangkok
page=30 block=-677445626 (x=191 y=1) width=289.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
181
page=30 block=470679567 (x=513 y=3) width=14.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
became renowned for his murals at Wat Bowonniwet
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=28) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
and Wat Boromniwat during the reign of King Mongkut.
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=41) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
First observations of the framed paintings at the
page=30 block=16524648 (x=19 y=54) width=234.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
monasteries described above suggest that after the China
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=67) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
trade waned in the mid-nineteenth century and glass
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=80) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
paintings ceased to be a popular item of import, there
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=92) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
was a shift to using paintings that were designed and
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=105) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
produced locally. They were hung in the same manner
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=118) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
as the Chinese paintings had been, typically in gilt
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=131) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
frames, often in trios, over the windows and doors of
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=144) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
temples, but the source of inspiration for medium and
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=157) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
style gradually changed from Chinese glass paintings to
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=170) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
European art. This parallels the broader shift of cultural
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=183) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
paradigms in Siam. The increasing presence of Euro-
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=196) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
peans and Americans in Siam and the 1851 succession
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=209) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
of King Mongkut, who studied Western culture with
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=222) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the same fervor that his predecessor had traded with
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=235) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
China, meant that new fashions and ideas began to be
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=248) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
drawn from occidental sources. These new sources of
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=261) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
in?uence and inspiration did not fully replace but rather
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=274) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
merged and combined with what had come before. If
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=287) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Thai Buddhism and its visual culture is characterized
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=300) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
by accretion, this is because it is loathe to discard the
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=313) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
material fabric already established in temple settings.
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=326) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
New donations add to those that came before, and the
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=339) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
pious clutter attests to both a history of rich patronage
page=30 block=16524648 (x=1 y=352) width=252.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=LINHFC+AdvPN10205C wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and the spiritual ethos of abundance.

stuff.’’
